They've been watching my moves and messing with the pallet counts on purpose, just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that your husband and coworkers are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. I mean, like I just said, I know what I'm seeing out there. It's not in my head. I see them watching me on the floor, and my pallet counts keep coming up wrong when I know exactly what I loaded. They're doing it on purpose so I don't get that supervisor job, and my husband just takes their side and calls me crazy. It just says exactly that... \"Nicole, check it again.\" I mean, I usually go back and count my boxes or check the pallet when I hear it, just to be sure. It all makes sense in my head, but people just look at me and say they have no idea what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Is your trouble expressing your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others something that has been happening since the voices and feeling of being watched started, or has it been a problem for you for a longer time?\n\nPatient: Well, the voices and that feeling that people are watching me or changing things around... that's been happening for about eight months now. But for the last eight months, it's just been constant.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned voices, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Well, it's just... sometimes when I'm in the aisle at work, or even just folding clothes at home, I hear this man's voice. It's clear as day, like he's standing right next to me. He just says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" But when I look around, there's nobody near me.\n\nDoctor: What does the voice tell you to do when it says 'Nicole, check it again'?\n\nPatient: It doesn't really tell me to do anything else. It just says exactly that... \"Nicole, check it again.\" I mean, I usually go back and count my boxes or check the pallet when I hear it, just to be sure. But the voice itself doesn't give me any other instructions or tell me what I'm supposed to be looking for. It's just that one sentence.\n\nDoctor: Is there one main idea or theme that you think is behind everything your husband, coworkers, and the voices are doing?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's pretty obvious to me. My husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy for saying it, but I know they've been watching my moves. They're the ones changing the pallet counts behind my back. And it's all just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. That's what this whole thing comes down to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feelings in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. When I try to explain what's going on to people, it's like... my words just run all over the place. I'll start out talking about the forklift sheets from work, and then I'm talking about the church signs, and then it jumps to my husband's tone of voice. I try to get it all out so they understand, but people just tell me they can't follow what I'm saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble expressing your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, it happens most of the time. Whenever I try to explain what's going on, my words just run together. I'll jump from the forklift sheets, straight to the church signs, and then over to my husband's tone. People just flat out tell me they can't follow what I'm saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to stay on one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's exactly like I was just trying to explain. I just can't seem to stay on track. I'll start trying to tell someone about the forklift sheets at the warehouse, and before I know it, I'm talking about the messages on the church signs, and then suddenly I'm going on about the way my husband talks to me. It all makes sense in my head, but people just look at me and say they have no idea what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Is your trouble expressing your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others something that has been happening since the voices and feeling of being watched started, or has it been a problem for you for a longer time?\n\nPatient: Well, the voices and that feeling that people are watching me or changing things around... that's been happening for about eight months now. I'd say the trouble with my words kind of lines up with that, yeah. It really just got all jumbled up like this over the last eight months or so.\n\nDoctor: Was your way of thinking and expressing yourself always a bit disorganized, or is this something that has changed over time?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I was just saying, it definitely changed. It wasn't always like this. I used to make sense to people just fine until about eight months ago when everything started getting messy.\n\nDoctor: Did you used to be able to express your thoughts in a clear and organized way before these problems started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I mean... I feel like I'm repeating myself a bit here, but yes. I could talk perfectly fine before all this started. Nobody had any trouble understanding me back then, it really just started getting bad eight months ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had some difficulty keeping your thoughts organized, even before the voices and feeling of being watched started?\n\nPatient: No, I really didn't. I know we just went over this a few times, but no... I didn't have any trouble keeping my thoughts straight before the voices and the feeling of being watched started. All of that—the messy thoughts, the voices, everything—it all just kind of hit me at the same time around eight months ago. Before that, my head was fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where your thoughts are more organized and easier to express, or is it consistently difficult for you to express your thoughts clearly?\n\nPatient: It's pretty much all the time now. I mean... I don't really have days where my head just clears up and I can talk normal again. Ever since it started eight months ago, it's just been a constant struggle to get the words out right. It doesn't really go away.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with organizing your thoughts before the last eight months?\n\nPatient: Like I said a minute ago... no, I didn't. I feel like we're kind of going in circles here, but I really never had any issues putting my thoughts together before those eight months. Everything was completely fine back then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. He just says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" But when I look around, there's nobody near me.\n\nDoctor: What does the voice tell you to do when it says 'Nicole, check it again'?\n\nPatient: It doesn't really tell me to do anything else. It just says exactly that... \"Nicole, check it again.\" I mean, I usually go back and count my boxes or check the pallet when I hear it, just to be sure. I'll start out talking about the forklift sheets from work, and then I'm talking about the church signs, and then it jumps to my husband's tone of voice. I try to get it all out so they understand, but people just tell me they can't follow what I'm saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble expressing your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, it happens most of the time. Whenever I try to explain what's going on, my words just run together. I'll jump from the forklift sheets, straight to the church signs, and then over to my husband's tone. People just flat out tell me they can't follow what I'm saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to stay on one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's exactly like I was just trying to explain. I just can't seem to stay on track. I'll start trying to tell someone about the forklift sheets at the warehouse, and before I know it, I'm talking about the messages on the church signs, and then suddenly I'm going on about the way my husband talks to me. It all makes sense in my head, but people just look at me and say they have no idea what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Is your trouble expressing your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others something that has been happening since the voices and feeling of being watched started, or has it been a problem for you for a longer time?\n\nPatient: Well, the voices and that feeling that people are watching me or changing things around... that's been happening for about eight months now.
